Human: Is this the best way with the best tool?

Claude: ## Your Current Approach: Strengths & Weaknesses

- Using DOB permits data is smart - it's a reliable source for finding active developers
- Airtable as a CRM is user-friendly and good for smaller datasets
- The deduplication by BIN is the right approach

- Scale limitations : Airtable can become expensive and slow with large datasets
- Email deliverability : Sending cold emails directly from Airtable automations often leads to spam folder placement
- Compliance risks : Cold emailing without proper setup can hurt your domain reputation
- Data freshness : Manual permit downloads won't keep your pipeline current

## Better Tools & Approach

### For CRM & Data Management:

- HubSpot or Pipedrive (free tiers available) - better for sales pipelines than Airtable
- PostgreSQL + Retool - if you want more control and lower costs at scale
- Clay.com - specifically designed for lead enrichment and outreach sequences

### For Email Outreach:

- Instantly.ai or Lemlist - purpose-built for cold email with better deliverability
- Apollo.io - combines CRM, email finder, and outreach in one platform
- Smartlead - good for multi-domain email warming and sequences

### For Data Collection:

- Set up automated scraping of DOB permits instead of manual downloads
- Use webhook notifications when new permits are filed (if available)

## Recommended Improved Workflow

1. Automated Data Collection : Set up a script to automatically pull new permits weekly/monthly
2. Lead Enrichment : Use tools like Apollo, ZoomInfo, or Clay to find contact info and company details
3. Email Warming : Use dedicated domains and warm them up before sending
4. Multi-channel Outreach : Combine email, LinkedIn, and phone in sequences
5. Better Tracking : Use tools that integrate with your CRM for reply tracking

## Quick Win Suggestions

- Use Zapier or Make.com instead of Airtable automations for more flexibility
- Set up dedicated sending domains for cold emails
- Add LinkedIn outreach to your sequences using tools like Sales Navigator
- Implement email verification before sending to improve deliverability

Claude: ## The Real Challenge: Multi-Source Data Integration

1. Monitor daily activity (DOB permits + DOB NOW permits)
2. Cross-reference for ownership (approved permits → owner names)
3. Determine management status (HPD, WhoOwnsWhat, historical projects)
4. Enrich & pursue only non-self-managed properties

## Recommended Tech Stack

### Data Pipeline & Processing:

- PostgreSQL database - handle complex joins and relationships
- Python scripts - automate data collection and cross-referencing
- Retool or Bubble - build custom dashboards and workflows
- Airtable or Notion - final CRM layer (easier for team use)

### For Data Collection:

- Selenium/Playwright - automate permit scraping
- API integrations where available (HPD has some APIs)
- Scheduled cloud functions (AWS Lambda/Google Cloud Functions)

## Proposed Workflow Architecture

### Phase 1: Data Collection & Processing

```
Daily Automated Pipeline:
├── Scrape DOB permits (new applications)
├── Scrape DOB NOW permits (new applications)  
├── Scrape approved permits (for owner names)
├── Cross-reference by BIN to match applications → owners
├── Query HPD database for management info
├── Check WhoOwnsWhat for additional context
├── Store all in PostgreSQL with proper relationships
```

### Phase 2: Lead Qualification

```
Automated Filtering:
├── Identify new owner names from recent permits
├── Check against historical database (previous projects)
├── Determine if self-managed or uses management company
├── Flag qualified leads (non-self-managed developers)
├── Export to CRM for outreach
```

### Phase 3: CRM & Outreach

- Clay.com - best for this type of complex lead enrichment
- Apollo.io - find contact info and manage outreach
- Instantly.ai - email sequences with proper deliverability

## Why This Setup Works Better

1. Handles complexity : PostgreSQL can manage the complex relationships between permits, owners, and properties
2. Scalable : Cloud functions can process thousands of records daily
3. Accurate : Cross-referencing ensures you're not missing connections
4. Efficient : Only qualified leads make it to the expensive CRM/outreach tools

## Implementation Steps

### Week 1-2: Database Setup

- Set up PostgreSQL with proper schema for permits, owners, properties, management companies
- Create automated scrapers for all data sources
- Build cross-referencing logic

### Week 3: Lead Qualification

- Build rules engine to identify non-self-managed developers
- Create automated filtering and scoring
- Set up exports to CRM

### Week 4: CRM Integration

- Connect to Clay/Apollo for contact enrichment
- Set up outreach sequences
- Build reporting dashboard

## Alternative: All-in-One Solution

- Clay.com can handle a lot of this data processing and enrichment
- Phantombuster for automated data collection
- Airtable as the final CRM layer

Human: I can also download or use the api to pull the building registrations, I thin the address of the company and entity name would be the best cross refrance, I uploaded all the sheetsinto clay but they aren't fully showing up. 2 of them are blank

Claude: ## Clay Upload Issues - Quick Fixes

### Common Clay Upload Problems:

1. File size limits - Clay has limits on CSV size
2. Column formatting - Special characters or date formats
3. Encoding issues - Non-UTF-8 files
4. Empty rows - Clay sometimes shows blank if there are formatting issues

### Immediate Troubleshooting Steps:

1. Check file sizes - Clay typically handles up to 50MB per file
2. Look at column headers - Make sure no special characters in headers
3. Check for empty rows at the top/bottom of your CSVs
4. Try uploading one file at a time to isolate the problem

## Better Cross-Reference Strategy

### Cross-Reference Hierarchy:

1. Entity Name (exact match) - Most reliable
2. Company Address (fuzzy match) - Catches variations
3. Owner Name (partial match) - For individual owners
4. BIN (backup) - When entity data is missing

`// Let's see what files are available
try {
    // Check for CSV versions
    const files = [
        'Registration_Contacts.csv',
        'DOB_NOW_Job_Application_Filings.csv', 
        'DOB_NOW_Approved_Permits.csv',
        'DOB_Job_Application_Filings.csv'
    ];
    
    for (const fileName of files) {
        try {
            const content = await window.fs.readFile(fileName, { encoding: 'utf8' });
            console.log(`✓ Found: ${fileName}`);
            console.log(`  Size: ${content.length} characters`);
            
            // Get first few lines to see structure
            const lines = content.split('\n').slice(0, 3);
            console.log(`  Headers: ${lines[0]}`);
            console.log(`  Sample: ${lines[1]}`);
            console.log('');
        } catch (e) {
            console.log(`✗ Not found: ${fileName}`);
        }
    }
} catch (error) {
    console.log("Error checking files:", error.message);
}`

`// Let's examine the Column Info sheet for DOB Job Applications
import * as XLSX from 'xlsx';

const dobDict = await window.fs.readFile('DD_DOB_Job_Application_Filings_20190619.xlsx');
const dobDictWorkbook = XLSX.read(dobDict);

// Get the Column Info sheet
const columnInfoSheet = dobDictWorkbook.Sheets['Column Info'];
const columnInfo = XLSX.utils.sheet_to_json(columnInfoSheet);

console.log("DOB Job Application Fields:");
console.log("==========================================");

columnInfo.forEach((row, i) => {
    if (row['Field Name']) {
        console.log(`${row['Field Name']}: ${row['Description']}`);
    }
});

console.log("\n\nKey fields for cross-referencing:");
console.log("==========================================");

// Filter for key fields we need
const keyFields = columnInfo.filter(row => {
    const fieldName = row['Field Name']?.toLowerCase() || '';
    return fieldName.includes('owner') || 
           fieldName.includes('applicant') || 
           fieldName.includes('bin') || 
           fieldName.includes('address') ||
           fieldName.includes('entity') ||
           fieldName.includes('business');
});

keyFields.forEach(field => {
    console.log(`• ${field['Field Name']}: ${field['Description']}`);
});`

Row 1: Column Name | Column Description | Additional Notes

Row 2: Job # | The DOB Job Application Number assigned when the applicant begins the application. | This is the unique identifier for the application submitted to the Department. It may contain several work types, and more work types may be added as the application review and the work continues. It is a 9-digit number where the first digit indicates the borough where the building is located. 
• 1 = Manhattan
• 2 = Bronx
• 3 = Brooklyn
• 4 = Queens
• 5 = Staten Island
Row 3: Doc # | A sequential number assigned to each of the documents that make up a job application. | Every job application should have a 01 document. Every additional document receives a number that increases by 1 (ex: 02, 03, 04)
Row 4: Borough | The name of the NYC borough where the proposed work will take place. | Expected values = MANHATTAN, BRONX, BROOKLYN, QUEENS, STATEN ISLAND.
Row 5: House # | The house number for the building where the proposed work will take place. | In the address, "280 Broadway", 280 is the house number. This number may contain a dash (-), as in "8-15 27 Avenue".
Row 6: Street Name | The street name for the building where the proposed work will take place. | In the address, "1050 Park Place", Park Place is the street name. 
Row 7: Block | Tax Block assigned to the location of the proposed work by the Department of Finance. | undefined
Row 8: Lot | Tax Lot assigned to the location of the proposed work by the Department of Finance. | undefined
Row 9: Bin # | Building Identification Number assigned by Department of City Planning. | This BIN comes from the DOB database.
Row 10: Job Type | 2-digit code to indicate the overall job type for the application. | Expected values are:
• A1 = Alteration Type I, A major alteration that will change the use, egress, or occupancy of the building.
• A2 = Alteration Type II, An application with multiple types of work that do not affect the use, egress, or occupancy of the building.
• A3 = Alteration Type III, One type of minor work that doesn't affect the use, egress, or occupancy of the building.
• NB = New Building, An application to build a new structure. “NB” cannot be selected if any existing building elements are to remain—for example a part of
an old foundation, a portion of a façade that will be incorporated into the construction, etc.
• DM = Demolition, An application to fully or partially demolish an existing building.
• PA = A Place of Assembly (PA) Certificate of Operation is required for premises where 75 or more members of the public gather indoors or 200 or more gather outdoors, for religious, recreational, educational, political, or social purposes, or to consume food or drink. This type of job filing is related to a NB or A1.
• SC = Subdivision Condominiums, The division of a tax lot into several smaller tax lots allowing each condominium to have its own tax lot.
• SI = Subdivision Improved, An improved subdivision is when one lot is being broken into several smaller lots. The Department of Finance must assign new lot numbers to subdivisions.
Row 11: Job Status | A single letter to indicate the overall status for the job application. | See the Building Information System: Job Status and Permit Types for expected values: http://www1.nyc.gov/assets/buildings/pdf/bisjobstatus.pdf
Row 12: Job Status Descrp | The description of the job status letter code. | See the Building Information System: Job Status and Permit Types for expected values: http://www1.nyc.gov/assets/buildings/pdf/bisjobstatus.pdf
Row 13: Latest Action Date | The date of the last update to the DOB record for the job filing. | Format: MM/DD/YYYY
Row 14: Building Type | Legal occupancy classification. | Expected values are:
• 1-2-3 Family
• Other

DOF classification has 36 types - this field just simplifies it for DOB purposes. Anything above 3 family we consider multiple dwelling and is regulated more heavily. Other agencies might have different cutoffs.
Row 15: Community - Board | Community Board Number for the building’s address. | undefined
Row 16: Cluster | This indicates that a job filing is part of a housing development where all of the buildings will be built exactly the same way. There is a main job folder with all of the paperwork and plans. This was a common practice when there was a development boom, but is not common anymore. | Expected values are D, N, P, Y
Row 17: Landmarked | Indicates that the building has been designated as a Landmark Building by the Landmarks Preservation Commission. | Expected values are:

Little “E” or RD Site: Anytime there is an environmental cleanup in the City of New York, the location is identified as “E” on DCP zoning maps. Row 42: Pre- Filing Date | This is a date. This is the first step in the process for all job applications. The job application # is assigned at this status. This occurs when the applicant submits any part of the application (even a single form), in person or electronically.  | Job Status A
Row 43: Paid | This is a date. This only applies to manual filings. Payment for the job was made to D.E.A.R., the Data Entry and Research Unit. | Job Status C
Row 44: Fully Paid | This is a date. It indicates that all data entry is complete and payments have been made. | Job Status D
Row 45: Assigned | This is a date. This indicates that the Assistant/Chief Plan Examiner (A/CPE) has assigned the job to a plan examiner. | Job Status F
Row 46: Approved | This is a date. This indicates that the entire job has been approved by the Plan Examiner. The applicant can now pull a permit. | Job Status P
Row 47: Fully Permitted | This is a date. This indicates that all permits have been pulled. | Job Status R
Row 48: Initial Cost | This is a dollar amount that indicates the applicant's estimate for how much the job will cost. | Estimated Job Cost on the PW1
Row 49: Total Est. Fee | This is a dollar amount that indicates our calculation for the fee after Fee Estimation Review. | undefined`

`// Let's continue looking for ownership fields in DOB data
import * as XLSX from 'xlsx';

const dobDict = await window.fs.readFile('DD_DOB_Job_Application_Filings_20190619.xlsx');
const dobDictWorkbook = XLSX.read(dobDict);
const columnSheet = dobDictWorkbook.Sheets['Column Info'];
const rawColumnData = XLSX.utils.sheet_to_json(columnSheet, { header: 1 });

console.log("=== SEARCHING FOR OWNERSHIP FIELDS IN DOB DATA ===");

// Look for rows containing owner/applicant information
for (let i = 0; i < rawColumnData.length; i++) {
    const row = rawColumnData[i];
    if (row && row[0] && typeof row[0] === 'string') {
        const fieldName = row[0].toLowerCase();
        if (fieldName.includes('owner') || fieldName.includes('applicant') || fieldName.includes('business') || fieldName.includes('entity')) {
            console.log(`${row[0]}: ${row[1]}`);
        }
    }
}

console.log("\n=== KEY FIELDS FOR CROSS-REFERENCING ===");
console.log("BIN #: Building Identification Number");
console.log("Applicant's First Name: First name of person who filed"); 
console.log("Applicant's Last Name: Last name of person who filed");
console.log("Applicant Professional Title: License type");
console.log("Applicant License #: License number");

// Now let's check the HPD Registration structure from the PDF
console.log("\n=== HPD REGISTRATION FIELDS (from PDF) ===");
console.log("Key fields for cross-referencing:");
console.log("• RegistrationID: Unique identifier");
console.log("• BuildingID (BIN): Building identifier");
console.log("• RegistrationContact Type: 'Individual Owner', 'Corporate Owner', 'Managing Agent', etc.");
console.log("• CorporationName: If applicable");
console.log("• FirstName, LastName: Contact person");
console.log("• BusinessHouseNumber, BusinessStreetName, etc.: Contact address");

console.log("\n=== CROSS-REFERENCE STRATEGY ===");
console.log("1. Match by BIN (Building ID) - most reliable");
console.log("2. Match by entity name (CorporationName vs business name)");
console.log("3. Match by contact address");
console.log("4. Use RegistrationContact Type to identify management structure");`

## Step 2: Create Master Cross-Reference Table

### Start with DOB_NOW_Approved_Permits as your base:

1. Open DOB_NOW_Approved_Permits table
2. Add new column: "HPD Registration Lookup"
3. Use this formula:

```
=VLOOKUP([BIN #], HPD_Registrations, "BuildingID", "RegistrationID,HouseNumber,StreetName,Boro")
```

## Step 3: Add Management Company Lookup

1. Add another column: "Management Info"
2. Use this formula:

```
=VLOOKUP([HPD Registration Lookup.RegistrationID], Registration_Contacts, "RegistrationID", "Type,CorporationName,FirstName,LastName")
```

## Step 4: Create Qualification Logic

1. Add column: "Lead Score"
2. Use this formula:

```
let score = 0;
let reasons = [];

// Check management type
const mgmtType = [Management Info.Type];
if (mgmtType === "Managing Agent") {
  score += 50;
  reasons.push("Uses external management");
} else if (mgmtType === "Corporate Owner") {
  score += 20;
  reasons.push("Corporate owner");
}

// Check project value
const cost = parseFloat([Initial Cost]) || 0;
if (cost > 500000) {
  score += 30;
  reasons.push("High-value project: $" + cost.toLocaleString());
}

return {
  score: score,
  qualified: score >= 50,
  reasons: reasons.join("; ")
};
```

Claude: ## Project Summary for Lovable

### What We're Building:

### Data Sources We Have:

1. DOB_NOW_Approved_Permits - Contains BIN numbers and reliable owner names/entities
2. DOB_NOW_Job_Application_Filings - Recent permit applications with project details
3. DOB_Job_Application_Filings - Traditional permit applications
4. HPD_Registrations - Building registration data with BIN numbers and RegistrationIDs
5. Registration_Contacts - Contact details linked by RegistrationID, shows if owner uses "Managing Agent" vs "Corporate Owner"

### Cross-Reference Logic Needed:

```
DOB Permits (BIN) → HPD Registrations (BuildingID = BIN) → Registration_Contacts (RegistrationID)
```

### Key Fields to Match:

- BIN numbers (Building IDs) across DOB and HPD data
- RegistrationID to link HPD building data with contact info
- Owner names/entities to build portfolios per owner

### Output We Want:

- Owner name/entity
- All properties they own (BIN numbers and addresses)
- Management status (self-managed vs uses management company)
- Recent activity (permits, project values)
- Lead qualification score (based on portfolio size, management status, activity)
- Contact information for outreach

### Business Logic:

- High-value targets: Use management companies + have multiple properties + recent activity
- Portfolio analysis: Group all properties by owner to show their full portfolio
- Qualification scoring: Points for external management (50pts), portfolio size (20-40pts), recent activity (10-20pts), project value (10-30pts)

### Technical Requirements:

- Data type handling: All IDs should be text (not numbers) to avoid formatting issues
- Cross-referencing: VLOOKUP or similar across 5 tables
- Deduplication: Each owner appears once with their full portfolio
- Export capability: Final qualified leads exported for CRM integration

Claude: ## Complete NYC Developer Lead Generation System - Project Brief for Lovable

### Business Overview:

### Complete Workflow:

1. Data Collection → Cross-reference government databases

## Phase 1: Data Processing & Cross-Referencing

### Data Sources (5 CSV files):

1. DOB_NOW_Approved_Permits - Most reliable owner names, BIN numbers, project approvals
2. DOB_NOW_Job_Application_Filings - Recent permit applications, project values, filing dates
3. DOB_Job_Application_Filings - Traditional permits, historical activity
4. HPD_Registrations - Building registrations with BIN→RegistrationID mapping
5. Registration_Contacts - Contact info and management structure (Owner vs Managing Agent)

### Cross-Reference Logic:

```
Step 1: DOB Permits (BIN) → HPD Registrations (BuildingID = BIN) 
Step 2: HPD Registrations (RegistrationID) → Registration_Contacts (RegistrationID)
Step 3: Group by Owner → Build complete portfolios
```

### Key Data Points to Extract:

- BIN numbers (building identifiers)
- Owner names/business entities
- Property addresses and boroughs
- Project values and dates
- Management type ("Managing Agent" = uses external management)
- Contact information (names, addresses, phones)

## Phase 2: Lead Qualification Engine

### Qualification Scoring (0-100 points):

- Uses "Managing Agent": +50 points (prime target)
- "Corporate Owner": +20 points
- "Individual Owner": +10 points

- 10+ properties: +40 points
- 5-9 properties: +30 points
- 2-4 properties: +20 points
- 1 property: +5 points

- 3+ permits: +20 points
- 1-2 permits: +10 points
- High project values (>$1M): +15 points
- Medium values ($500K-$1M): +10 points

- Multiple properties same borough: +10 points
- Manhattan properties: +15 points

### Target Thresholds:

- 70+ points: HIGH priority (immediate outreach)
- 50-69 points: MEDIUM priority (standard sequence)
- <50 points: LOW priority (nurture campaign)

## Phase 3: Contact Enrichment

### Data Enhancement Needed:

- Email addresses (primary contact method)
- Direct phone numbers (for follow-up calls)
- LinkedIn profiles (social selling)
- Company websites (research)

### Enrichment Sources:

- Use APIs like Apollo.io, ZoomInfo, or Hunter.io
- Cross-reference with business databases
- Social media lookup for individuals

## Phase 4: CRM & Outreach Automation

### CRM Structure:

- Owner/Entity Name
- Total Portfolio (property count)

### Data Architecture:

- Primary key: BIN number for properties, Owner ID for entities
- Relationships: One-to-many (Owner → Properties), Many-to-many (Properties → Permits)
- Data types: All IDs as text (avoid number formatting issues)
- Performance: Handle 10K+ properties, 1K+ owners efficiently

- DOB NOW API : https://data.cityofnewyork.us/resource/w9ak-pvkd.json
- HPD Registrations API : https://data.cityofnewyork.us/resource/tesw-yqqr.json
- HPD Contacts API : https://data.cityofnewyork.us/resource/feu5-w2e2.json

## n8n Workflow for Daily Updates:

### Node Setup:

1. Schedule Trigger - Daily at 6 AM
2. HTTP Request - GET from NYC APIs
3. Data Processing - Clean and format
4. Clay Webhook - Send to Clay for cross-referencing
5. HubSpot Integration - Update/create contacts
6. Slack/Email Alert - Daily summary

### API Queries for Latest Data:

```
// Get yesterday's permits
const yesterday = new Date(Date.now() - 24*60*60*1000).toISOString().split('T')[0];

// DOB Permits API call
GET https://data.cityofnewyork.us/resource/ipu4-2q9a.json?$where=latest_action_date='${yesterday}'

// DOB NOW API call  
GET https://data.cityofnewyork.us/resource/w9ak-pvkd.json?$where=latest_action_date='${yesterday}'
```

## Implementation Options:

### Option 1: Full n8n Automation (Recommended)

- Pro : Complete automation, no manual work
- Con : Requires n8n setup and API knowledge
- Cost : FREE (self-hosted) or $20/month (cloud)

### Option 2: Clay + Manual Updates

- Pro : Simple, use Clay's built-in tools
- Con : Manual daily/weekly uploads
- Cost : Just Clay subscription

### Option 3: Hybrid Approach

- Daily : Automated new permit alerts via n8n
- Weekly : Full data refresh in Clay
- Pro : Best of both worlds
- Con : More complex setup

- Incremental updates : Only pull new/changed records

```
// Project Details
const projectInfo = {
  bin: [Bin],
  address: [House No] + " " + [Street Name] + ", " + [Borough],
  borough: [Borough],
  proposed_units: parseInt([Proposed Dwelling Units]) || 0,
  project_value: parseFloat([Initial Cost]) || 0,
  filing_date: [Filing Date],
  current_status: [Filing Status],
  job_type: [Job Type]
};

// Revenue calculation (what's your monthly fee per unit?)
const monthlyFee = 50; // CHANGE THIS TO YOUR ACTUAL FEE
const monthlyRevenue = projectInfo.proposed_units * monthlyFee;
const annualRevenue = monthlyRevenue * 12;

return {
  ...projectInfo,
  qualifies_size: projectInfo.proposed_units >= 10,
  monthly_revenue_potential: monthlyRevenue,
  annual_revenue_potential: annualRevenue,
  project_age_months: Math.floor((new Date() - new Date(projectInfo.filing_date)) / (1000 * 60 * 60 * 24 * 30))
};
```

Claude: ## How to Add Formulas in Clay:

### Step 1: Add a New Column

1. Open your DOB_NOW_Job_Application_Filings table
2. Scroll to the right of your existing columns
3. Click the "+" button to add a new column
4. Name it: "Current Project Details"

### Step 2: Choose Formula Type

- Select "Formula" or "Enrichment"
- NOT "Text" or "Number"

### Step 3: Use Clay's Formula Syntax

```
{
  "bin": [Bin],
  "address": CONCATENATE([House No], " ", [Street Name], ", ", [Borough]),
  "borough": [Borough], 
  "proposed_units": [Proposed Dwelling Units],
  "project_value": [Initial Cost],
  "filing_date": [Filing Date],
  "monthly_revenue": MULTIPLY([Proposed Dwelling Units], 50),
  "annual_revenue": MULTIPLY([Proposed Dwelling Units], 600),
  "qualifies": IF([Proposed Dwelling Units] >= 10, "YES", "NO")
}
```

### Step 4: Alternative - Use Clay's Built-in Actions

1. Add column → "Enrichment"
2. Choose "Lookup" or "Find/Replace"
3. Set up the lookup between tables

Claude: ## Master Combined Table Strategy

### Step 1: Create "Master Under Construction" Table

1. Start with DOB_NOW_Job_Application_Filings as base
2. Add all rows from DOB_Job_Application_Filings
3. Standardize column names for unified processing

### Column Mapping for Combined Table:

```
Master ColumnDOB NOW SourceTraditional DOB SourceJob_NumberJob Filing NumberJob #BINBinBin #AddressHouse No + Street NameHouse # + Street NameBoroughBoroughBoroughJob_TypeJob TypeJob TypeUnits_ProposedProposed Dwelling UnitsProposed Dwelling UnitsProject_ValueInitial CostInitial CostFiling_DateFiling DatePre- Filing DateStatusFiling StatusJob Status
```

### Step 2: Clay Formula for Combined Processing

```
// Standardize data from both sources
{
  "job_number": [Job Filing Number] || [Job #],
  "bin": [Bin] || [Bin #], 
  "address": ([House No] || [House #]) + " " + [Street Name] + ", " + [Borough],
  "borough": [Borough],
  "job_type": [Job Type],
  "units_proposed": [Proposed Dwelling Units],
  "project_value": [Initial Cost],
  "filing_date": [Filing Date] || [Pre- Filing Date],
  "status": [Filing Status] || [Job Status],
  "source": [Filing Date] ? "DOB_NOW" : "TRADITIONAL_DOB"
}
```

### Step 3: Cross-Reference Chain

```
// Look up owner from approved permits
const bin = [Standardized Project Info].bin;

// Try DOB NOW Approved Permits first
let owner = VLOOKUP(bin, DOB_NOW_Approved_Permits, "Bin", "Owner Business Name,Owner Name,Owner Street Address");

// If not found and this is traditional DOB, try other approved sources
if (!owner && [Standardized Project Info].source === "TRADITIONAL_DOB") {
  // Add lookup to traditional approved permits if you have that table
  owner = VLOOKUP(bin, [Traditional_Approved_Permits], "Bin", "Owner Business Name,Owner Name,Owner Address");
}

return {
  "owner_name": owner["Owner Business Name"] || owner["Owner Name"],
  "owner_address": owner["Owner Street Address"] || owner["Owner Address"],
  "data_source": owner ? "FOUND" : "NOT_FOUND"
};
```

```
// Cross-reference with registrations
const bin = [Standardized Project Info].bin;

// Find registration ID
const regId = VLOOKUP(bin, Multiple_Dwelling_Registrations, "BIN", "RegistrationID");

if (!regId) {
  return {
    "registration_status": "NOT_YET_REGISTERED",
    "management_status": "NO_MANAGEMENT_CHOSEN",
    "target_priority": "HIGH"
  };
}

// If registered, check management
const management = VLOOKUP(regId, Registration_Contacts, "RegistrationID", "Type,CorporationName");

return {
  "registration_status": "ALREADY_REGISTERED", 
  "management_company": management.CorporationName,
  "management_type": management.Type,
  "target_priority": "LOW" // Already has management
};
```

### Step 4: Lead Qualification

```
const units = [Standardized Project Info].units_proposed;
const regStatus = [Registration Analysis].registration_status;

// Size filter
if (units < 10) {
  return { qualified: false, reason: "Less than 10 units" };
}

// Registration status (key filter)
if (regStatus === "ALREADY_REGISTERED") {
  return { qualified: false, reason: "Already has management" };
}

// Calculate score
let score = 0;
score += units >= 50 ? 40 : units >= 25 ? 30 : 20; // Size points
score += regStatus === "NOT_YET_REGISTERED" ? 40 : 0; // No management chosen
score += [Owner Information].data_source === "FOUND" ? 20 : 0; // Has owner info

return {
  qualified: score >= 60,
  score: score,
  priority: score >= 80 ? "HIGH" : score >= 60 ? "MEDIUM" : "LOW",
  monthly_revenue: units * 50, // Your fee per unit
  annual_revenue: units * 600
};
```

Claude: # NYC Developer Lead Generation System - Project Requirements

## Core Business Objective:

## Target Audience:

- Real estate developers building 10+ unit residential buildings
- Properties currently under construction (not yet registered with management)
- Historical database covering last 10 years of development activity

## Key Intelligence Required:

### Developer/Owner Information:

- Complete contact details (phone, email, office address)
- Business entity names and individual owner names
- Development history and portfolio size

### Property Portfolio Analysis:

- All buildings owned/developed by each entity
- Current projects under construction
- Completed projects and their management companies
- Pattern analysis of management choices

### Project Details:

- Property addresses and unit counts
- Construction status and estimated completion
- Project values and permit timelines
- Revenue potential calculations

### Management Intelligence:

- Current management companies used
- Self-managed vs. externally managed properties
- Management company switching patterns
- Competitive landscape analysis

## Data Sources:

- DOB Job Application Filings (traditional permits)
- DOB NOW Job Application Filings (newer permit system)
- DOB NOW Approved Permits (reliable owner information)
- Multiple Dwelling Registrations (registration status)
- Registration Contacts (management company relationships)

## Core System Features:

- job_type (text)
- proposed_units (integer)

Human: i have the account

Claude: ## Step 2: Create Database Tables in Supabase (15 minutes)

### In Supabase Dashboard:

1. Go to "SQL Editor" (left sidebar)
2. Click "New Query"
3. Copy and paste this SQL:

```
-- Table 1: Permit Applications (your 800K records)
CREATE TABLE permit_applications (
    id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    job_number TEXT,
    bin TEXT,
    borough TEXT,
    house_number TEXT,
    street_name TEXT,
    full_address TEXT,
    job_type TEXT,
    proposed_units INTEGER,
    existing_units INTEGER,
    filing_date DATE,
    project_value NUMERIC,
    job_status TEXT,
    owner_business_name TEXT,
    owner_first_name TEXT,
    owner_last_name TEXT,
    owner_address TEXT,
    owner_phone TEXT,
    source_system TEXT, -- 'DOB' or 'DOB_NOW'
    raw_data JSONB,
    created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW()
);

-- Table 2: Approved Permits (for owner lookup)
CREATE TABLE approved_permits (
    id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    job_number TEXT,
    bin TEXT,
    owner_business_name TEXT,
    owner_name TEXT,
    owner_address TEXT,
    approved_date DATE,
    source_system TEXT,
    raw_data JSONB,
    created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW()
);

-- Table 3: Building Registrations
CREATE TABLE building_registrations (
    id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    registration_id TEXT,
    building_id TEXT,
    bin TEXT,
    borough TEXT,
    house_number TEXT,
    street_name TEXT,
    registration_date DATE,
    registration_end_date DATE,
    raw_data JSONB,
    created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW()
);

-- Table 4: Registration Contacts
CREATE TABLE registration_contacts (
    id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    registration_contact_id TEXT,
    registration_id TEXT,
    contact_type TEXT, -- 'Individual Owner', 'Corporate Owner', 'Managing Agent'
    corporation_name TEXT,
    first_name TEXT,
    last_name TEXT,
    business_address TEXT,
    business_city TEXT,
    business_state TEXT,
    business_zip TEXT,
    raw_data JSONB,
    created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW()
);

-- Table 5: Processed Leads (final output)
CREATE TABLE qualified_leads (
    id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    bin TEXT,
    property_address TEXT,
    owner_name TEXT,
    owner_business_name TEXT,
    proposed_units INTEGER,
    project_value NUMERIC,
    portfolio_size INTEGER,
    management_status TEXT, -- 'SELF_MANAGED', 'EXTERNAL', 'UNKNOWN'
    management_company TEXT,
    qualification_score INTEGER,
    priority TEXT, -- 'HIGH', 'MEDIUM', 'LOW'
    revenue_potential NUMERIC,
    created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT NOW()
);

-- Create indexes for fast lookups
CREATE INDEX idx_permit_applications_bin ON permit_applications(bin);
CREATE INDEX idx_approved_permits_bin ON approved_permits(bin);
CREATE INDEX idx_building_registrations_bin ON building_registrations(bin);
CREATE INDEX idx_registration_contacts_reg_id ON registration_contacts(registration_id);
CREATE INDEX idx_permit_applications_units ON permit_applications(proposed_units);
CREATE INDEX idx_permit_applications_filing_date ON permit_applications(filing_date);
```

### Click "RUN" to execute the SQL

Claude: ## Step 3: Python Import Script

### Install Required Libraries:

```
pip install pandas supabase python-dotenv

### Create Import Script:

import_data.py

```
import pandas as pd
from supabase import create_client, Client
import numpy as np
from datetime import datetime

# Your Supabase credentials (replace with your actual values)
SUPABASE_URL = "YOUR_SUPABASE_URL"
SUPABASE_KEY = "YOUR_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY"

supabase: Client = create_client(SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_KEY)

def import_dob_permits():
    print("Loading DOB_Job_Application_Filings...")
    df = pd.read_csv("DOB_Job_Application_Filings.csv")
    
    print(f"Loaded {len(df)} rows")
    
    # Map to our table structure
    mapped_data = []
    
    for index, row in df.iterrows():
        if index % 10000 == 0:
            print(f"Processing row {index}")
        
        # Combine owner address
        owner_address = ""
        if pd.notna(row.get("Owner's House Number")):
            owner_address += str(row["Owner's House Number"]) + " "
        if pd.notna(row.get("Owner'sHouse Street Name")):
            owner_address += str(row["Owner'sHouse Street Name"])
        
        # Create full address
        full_address = ""
        if pd.notna(row.get("House #")):
            full_address += str(row["House #"]) + " "
        if pd.notna(row.get("Street Name")):
            full_address += str(row["Street Name"])
        if pd.notna(row.get("Borough")):
            full_address += ", " + str(row["Borough"])
        
        record = {
            "job_number": str(row.get("Job #", "")),
            "bin": str(row.get("Bin #", "")),
            "borough": str(row.get("Borough", "")),
            "house_number": str(row.get("House #", "")),
            "street_name": str(row.get("Street Name", "")),
            "full_address": full_address,
            "job_type": str(row.get("Job Type", "")),
            "proposed_units": int(row["Proposed Dwelling Units"]) if pd.notna(row.get("Proposed Dwelling Units")) else None,
            "existing_units": int(row["Existing Dwelling Units"]) if pd.notna(row.get("Existing Dwelling Units")) else None,
            "filing_date": row.get("Pre- Filing Date"),
            "project_value": float(row["Initial Cost"]) if pd.notna(row.get("Initial Cost")) else None,
            "job_status": str(row.get("Job Status", "")),
            "owner_business_name": str(row.get("Owner's Business Name", "")),
            "owner_first_name": str(row.get("Owner's First Name", "")),
            "owner_last_name": str(row.get("Owner's Last Name", "")),
            "owner_address": owner_address.strip(),
            "owner_phone": str(row.get("Owner'sPhone #", "")),
            "source_system": "DOB"
        }
        
        mapped_data.append(record)
    
    # Insert in chunks
    chunk_size = 1000
    for i in range(0, len(mapped_data), chunk_size):
        chunk = mapped_data[i:i+chunk_size]
        try:
            result = supabase.table("permit_applications").insert(chunk).execute()
            print(f"Inserted chunk {i//chunk_size + 1}/{(len(mapped_data)//chunk_size) + 1}")
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"Error inserting chunk {i//chunk_size + 1}: {e}")
            break
    
    print("DOB import complete!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    import_dob_permits()
```

### Setup Instructions:

1. Get your Supabase credentials:
   - Go to Supabase → Settings → API
   - Copy your Project URL and anon public key
2. Replace credentials in script:
   - SUPABASE_URL = "your-project-url"
   - SUPABASE_KEY = "your-anon-key"
3. Put script in same folder as your CSV files

);

-- Create indexes for important columns

CREATE INDEX idx_permit_units ON permit_applications(proposed_dwelling_units);
CREATE INDEX idx_permit_date ON permit_applications(pre_filing_date);
CREATE INDEX idx_permit_owner ON permit_applications(owner_business_name);

CREATE TABLE master_leads (
    id BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
    bin TEXT,
    property_address TEXT,
    borough TEXT,
    unit_count INTEGER,

Claude: ## STEP 16: Python Chunked Import for Large File

### Create Import Script:

import_large_file.py

```
import pandas as pd
from supabase import create_client, Client
import time

# Your Supabase credentials (replace with your actual values)
SUPABASE_URL = "YOUR_SUPABASE_URL"
SUPABASE_KEY = "YOUR_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY"

supabase: Client = create_client(SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_KEY)

def import_dob_permits_chunked():
    print("Loading DOB_Permit_Issuance.csv in chunks...")
    
    chunk_size = 10000  # Process 10K rows at a time
    chunk_num = 0
    
    # Read CSV in chunks
    for chunk in pd.read_csv("DOB_Permit_Issuance.csv", chunksize=chunk_size):
        chunk_num += 1
        print(f"Processing chunk {chunk_num} ({len(chunk)} rows)")
        
        # Convert to records for Supabase
        records = chunk.to_dict('records')
        
        # Insert chunk
        try:
            result = supabase.table("dob_permit_issuance").insert(records).execute()
            print(f"✅ Chunk {chunk_num} inserted successfully")
            
            # Small delay to be nice to the API
            time.sleep(2)
            
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"❌ Error inserting chunk {chunk_num}: {e}")
            break
    
    print("Import complete!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    import_dob_permits_chunked()
```

### Setup:

1. Get your Supabase credentials:
   - Go to Supabase → Settings → API
   - Copy Project URL and anon public key
2. Replace in script:
   - SUPABASE_URL = "your-url"
   - SUPABASE_KEY = "your-key"
3. Install pandas and supabase:  bashpip install pandas supabase

`SUPABASE_URL = "your-url"`

`SUPABASE_KEY = "your-key"`

```
pip install pandas supabase

import pandas as pd
from supabase import create_client, Client
import time

# Your Supabase credentials (replace with your actual values)
SUPABASE_URL = "YOUR_SUPABASE_URL"
SUPABASE_KEY = "YOUR_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY"

supabase: Client = create_client(SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_KEY)

def import_dob_permits_chunked():
    print("Loading DOB_Permit_Issuance.csv in chunks...")
    
    chunk_size = 10000  # Process 10K rows at a time
    chunk_num = 0
    
    # Read CSV in chunks
    for chunk in pd.read_csv("DOB_Permit_Issuance.csv", chunksize=chunk_size):
        chunk_num += 1
        print(f"Processing chunk {chunk_num} ({len(chunk)} rows)")
        
        # Convert to records for Supabase
        records = chunk.to_dict('records')
        
        # Insert chunk
        try:
            result = supabase.table("dob_permit_issuance").insert(records).execute()

```
import pandas as pd
from supabase import create_client, Client
import time

# Your Supabase credentials (replace with your actual values)
SUPABASE_URL = "YOUR_SUPABASE_URL"
SUPABASE_KEY = "YOUR_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY"

supabase: Client = create_client(SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_KEY)

def import_dob_permits_chunked():
    print("Loading DOB_Permit_Issuance.csv in chunks...")
    
    chunk_size = 10000  # Process 10K rows at a time
    chunk_num = 0
    
    # Read CSV in chunks
    for chunk in pd.read_csv("DOB_Permit_Issuance.csv", chunksize=chunk_size):
        chunk_num += 1
        print(f"Processing chunk {chunk_num} ({len(chunk)} rows)")
        
        # Convert to records for Supabase
        records = chunk.to_dict('records')
        
        # Insert chunk
        try:
            result = supabase.table("dob_permit_issuance").insert(records).execute()
            print(f"✅ Chunk {chunk_num} inserted successfully")
            
            # Small delay to be nice to the API
            time.sleep(2)
            
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"❌ Error inserting chunk {chunk_num}: {e}")
            break
    
    print("Import complete!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    import_dob_permits_chunked()
```

Human: Authentication
Supabase works through a mixture of JWT and Key auth.
If no Authorization header is included, the API will assume that you are making a request with an anonymous user.
If an Authorization header is included, the API will "switch" to the role of the user making the request. See the User Management section for more details.
We recommend setting your keys as Environment Variables.
Client API Keys
Client keys allow "anonymous access" to your database, until the user has logged in. After logging in the keys will switch to the user's own login token.
In this documentation, we will refer to the key using the name SUPABASE_KEY.
We have provided you a Client Key to get started. You will soon be able to add as many keys as you like. You can find the anon key in the API Settings page.
CLIENT API KEY

Authorization

Authorization

SUPABASE_KEY

anon

```
const SUPABASE_KEY = 'SUPABASE_CLIENT_API_KEY'
```

Example usage

```
const SUPABASE_URL = "https://ijpfcygpcpvikgrtlkwr.supabase.co"
```

const supabase = createClient(SUPABASE_URL, process.env.SUPABASE_KEY);
Service Keys
Service keys have FULL access to your data, bypassing any security policies. Be VERY careful where you expose these keys. They should only be used on a server and never on a client or browser.
In this documentation, we will refer to the key using the name SERVICE_KEY.
We have provided you with a Service Key to get started. Soon you will be able to add as many keys as you like. You can find the service_role in the API Settings page.
SERVICE KEY

SERVICE_KEY

service_role

```
const SERVICE_KEY = 'SUPABASE_SERVICE_KEY'
```

```

const supabase = createClient(SUPABASE_URL, process.env.SERVICE_KEY);

### Step 1: Open Google Colab

### Step 2: Upload and Process File

```
# Upload the large CSV file
from google.colab import files
import pandas as pd

# Upload your DOB_Permit_Issuance.csv
uploaded = files.upload()

# Read the CSV
print("Loading CSV file...")
df = pd.read_csv('DOB_Permit_Issuance.csv')
print(f"Original file: {len(df)} rows")

# Convert date column to datetime for proper sorting
df['Issuance Date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['Issuance Date'], errors='coerce')

# Sort by BIN and Issuance Date (most recent first)
df_sorted = df.sort_values(['Bin #', 'Issuance Date'], ascending=[True, False])

# Keep only the most recent permit per BIN
df_latest = df_sorted.drop_duplicates(subset=['Bin #'], keep='first')

print(f"After deduplication: {len(df_latest)} rows")
print(f"Reduction: {len(df) - len(df_latest)} rows removed")

# Download the filtered file
df_latest.to_csv('DOB_Permit_Issuance_Latest.csv', index=False)
files.download('DOB_Permit_Issuance_Latest.csv')

print("Filtered file downloaded!")
```

### Step 3: Run the Code

1. Click the play button next to the code cell
2. When prompted, upload your DOB_Permit_Issuance.csv
3. Wait for processing (should be much faster than 323MB download)
4. Download the filtered file

Claude: ## Updated Colab Code (Handles Any Filename):

```
# Upload the large CSV file
from google.colab import files
import pandas as pd

# Upload your file
uploaded = files.upload()

# Get the actual filename from the upload
filename = list(uploaded.keys())[0]
print(f"Uploaded file: {filename}")

# Read the CSV
print("Loading CSV file...")
df = pd.read_csv(filename)
print(f"Original file: {len(df)} rows")

# Convert date column to datetime for proper sorting
df['Issuance Date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['Issuance Date'], errors='coerce')

# Sort by BIN and Issuance Date (most recent first)
df_sorted = df.sort_values(['Bin #', 'Issuance Date'], ascending=[True, False])

# Keep only the most recent permit per BIN
df_latest = df_sorted.drop_duplicates(subset=['Bin #'], keep='first')

print(f"After deduplication: {len(df_latest)} rows")
print(f"Reduction: {len(df) - len(df_latest)} rows removed")

# Download the filtered file
output_filename = 'DOB_Permit_Issuance_Latest.csv'
df_latest.to_csv(output_filename, index=False)
files.download(output_filename)

print("Filtered file downloaded!")
```

# Upload and process DOB Permit Issuance file

import pandas as pd

print(f"Processing file: {filename}")

# Read the CSV
print("Loading CSV file...")
df = pd.read_csv(filename)
print(f"Original file: {len(df)} rows")

# Convert date column to datetime for proper sorting
df['Issuance Date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['Issuance Date'], errors='coerce')

# Sort by BIN and Issuance Date (most recent first)
df_sorted = df.sort_values(['Bin #', 'Issuance Date'], ascending=[True, False])

# Keep only the most recent permit per BIN
df_latest = df_sorted.drop_duplicates(subset=['Bin #'], keep='first')

print(f"After deduplication: {len(df_latest)} rows")
print(f"Reduction: {len(df) - len(df_latest)} rows removed")

Claude: ## Column Mapping in Supabase:

### When you upload the CSV:

1. Go to Table Editor → dob_permit_issuance
2. Click "Insert" → "Import data from CSV"
3. Select your DOB_Permit_Issuance_Latest.csv
4. Look for these options:
   - "Column mapping" button/tab
   - "Map columns" option
   - "Custom mapping"
   - A dropdown next to each column name

### What to Look For:

- Two-column layout showing:
   - CSV Column → Database Column
- Dropdown menus to match columns
- "Skip column" options

### Alternative: Check Import Settings

- "Header row" checkbox (make sure it's checked)
- "First row contains headers" (should be ON)
- "Advanced options" or "More options"

### If You Can't Find Mapping:

Claude: ## Click "Configure import data" Section

1. Click the dropdown arrow next to "Configure import data"
2. This should expand to show column mapping options
3. Look for column mapping/matching options in that expanded section

## The "Data incompatible" Shows the Issue

- Map CSV columns to database columns
- Skip columns that don't match
- Rename columns during import

Human: lets try pythin

Claude: ## Create Python Import Script:

import_filtered_file.py

```
import pandas as pd
from supabase import create_client, Client
import time

# Your Supabase credentials
SUPABASE_URL = "https://ijpfcygpcpvikgrtlkwr.supabase.co"
SUPABASE_KEY = "your_actual_anon_key_here"  # Replace with your actual key

supabase: Client = create_client(SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_KEY)

def import_filtered_permits():
    print("Loading DOB_Permit_Issuance_Latest.csv...")
    
    # Read the filtered CSV
    df = pd.read_csv("DOB_Permit_Issuance_Latest.csv")
    print(f"Loaded {len(df)} rows")
    
    # Convert to records for Supabase
    records = df.to_dict('records')
    
    # Import in chunks
    chunk_size = 1000
    total_chunks = (len(records) + chunk_size - 1) // chunk_size
    
    for i in range(0, len(records), chunk_size):
        chunk = records[i:i+chunk_size]
        chunk_num = (i // chunk_size) + 1
        
        print(f"Importing chunk {chunk_num}/{total_chunks} ({len(chunk)} rows)")
        
        try:
            result = supabase.table("dob_permit_issuance").insert(chunk).execute()
            print(f"✅ Chunk {chunk_num} imported successfully")
            time.sleep(1)  # Be nice to the API
            
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"❌ Error importing chunk {chunk_num}: {e}")
            break
    
    print("Import complete!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    import_filtered_permits()
```

## Setup:

1. Save this as import_filtered_file.py (same folder as your CSV)
2. Replace "your_actual_anon_key_here" with your actual Supabase key
3. Make sure your DOB_Permit_Issuance_Latest.csv is in the same folder

`import_filtered_file.py`

`"your_actual_anon_key_here"`

`DOB_Permit_Issuance_Latest.csv`

## Run It:

```
cd /path/to/your/folder
python3 import_filtered_file.py
```

## Let's Try a Different Approach - Python Bulk Insert

bulk_import.py

```
import pandas as pd
from supabase import create_client, Client
import time

# Your Supabase credentials
SUPABASE_URL = "https://ijpfcygpcpvikgrtlkwr.supabase.co"
SUPABASE_KEY = "your_actual_anon_key_here"  # Replace with your key

supabase: Client = create_client(SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_KEY)

def bulk_import_permits():
    print("Loading CSV...")
    df = pd.read_csv("DOB_Permit_Issuance_Latest.csv")
    print(f"Loaded {len(df)} rows")
    
    # Convert DataFrame to list of dictionaries
    records = []
    for index, row in df.iterrows():
        if index % 10000 == 0:
            print(f"Processing row {index}")
        
        record = {}
        for col in df.columns:
            # Clean the data
            value = row[col]
            if pd.isna(value):
                value = ""
            else:
                value = str(value)
            record[col] = value
        
        records.append(record)
    
    print(f"Converted {len(records)} records")
    
    # Insert in chunks
    chunk_size = 1000
    for i in range(0, len(records), chunk_size):
        chunk = records[i:i+chunk_size]
        print(f"Inserting chunk {i//chunk_size + 1}")
        
        try:
            result = supabase.table("dob_permit_issuance").insert(chunk).execute()
            print(f"✅ Success")
            time.sleep(1)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"❌ Error: {e}")
            break
    
    print("Import complete!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    bulk_import_permits()
```

Human: This is the file name: DOB_Permit_Fixed (2).csv where do I paste this code?

Claude: ## Complete Python Script:

bulk_import.py

```
import pandas as pd
from supabase import create_client, Client
import time

# Your Supabase credentials
SUPABASE_URL = "https://ijpfcygpcpvikgrtlkwr.supabase.co"
SUPABASE_KEY = "eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiIsInR5cCI6IkpXVCJ9.eyJpc3MiOiJzdXBhYmFzZSIsInJlZiI6ImlqcGZjeWdwY3B2aWtncnRsa3dyIiwicm9sZSI6ImFub24iLCJpYXQiOjE3NTI2MzUwNjAsImV4cCI6MjA2ODIxMTA2MH0.8QnlCxewNzcEbj1ZiOmKLGAxbY6xOhna3ZWp1cS6q54"

supabase: Client = create_client(SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_KEY)

def bulk_import_permits():
    print("Loading CSV...")
    df = pd.read_csv("DOB_Permit_Fixed (2).csv")
    print(f"Loaded {len(df)} rows")
    
    # Convert DataFrame to list of dictionaries
    records = []
    for index, row in df.iterrows():
        if index % 10000 == 0:
            print(f"Processing row {index}")
        
        record = {}
        for col in df.columns:
            # Clean the data
            value = row[col]
            if pd.isna(value):
                value = ""
            else:
                value = str(value)
            record[col] = value
        
        records.append(record)
    
    print(f"Converted {len(records)} records")
    
    # Insert in chunks
    chunk_size = 1000
    for i in range(0, len(records), chunk_size):
        chunk = records[i:i+chunk_size]
        print(f"Inserting chunk {i//chunk_size + 1}")
        
        try:
            result = supabase.table("dob_permit_issuance").insert(chunk).execute()
            print(f"✅ Success")
            time.sleep(1)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"❌ Error: {e}")
            break
    
    print("Import complete!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    bulk_import_permits()
```

2. Run it in Terminal:  bashpython3 bulk_import.py

## Update bulk_import.py:

bulk_import.py

```
import pandas as pd
from supabase import create_client, Client
import time

# Your Supabase credentials
SUPABASE_URL = "https://ijpfcygpcpvikgrtlkwr.supabase.co"
SUPABASE_KEY = "eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiIsInR5cCI6IkpXVCJ9.eyJpc3MiOiJzdXBhYmFzZSIsInJlZiI6ImlqcGZjeWdwY3B2aWtncnRsa3dyIiwicm9sZSI6ImFub24iLCJpYXQiOjE3NTI2MzUwNjAsImV4cCI6MjA2ODIxMTA2MH0.8QnlCxewNzcEbj1ZiOmKLGAxbY6xOhna3ZWp1cS6q54"

supabase: Client = create_client(SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_KEY)

def bulk_import_permits():
    print("Loading CSV...")
    df = pd.read_csv("DOB_Permit_Fixed (2).csv", dtype=str, low_memory=False)
    print(f"Loaded {len(df)} rows")
    
    # Fill NaN values with empty strings
    df = df.fillna('')
    
    # Convert to records
    records = df.to_dict('records')
    print(f"Converted {len(records)} records")
    
    # Insert in chunks
    chunk_size = 1000
    for i in range(0, len(records), chunk_size):
        chunk = records[i:i+chunk_size]
        print(f"Inserting chunk {i//chunk_size + 1}")
        
        try:
            result = supabase.table("dob_permit_issuance").insert(chunk).execute()
            print(f"✅ Success")
            time.sleep(1)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"❌ Error: {e}")
            break
    
    print("Import complete!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    bulk_import_permits()
```

Claude: ## Create Simplified Import Script:

simple_import.py

```
import pandas as pd
from supabase import create_client, Client
import time

# Your Supabase credentials
SUPABASE_URL = "https://ijpfcygpcpvikgrtlkwr.supabase.co"
SUPABASE_KEY = "eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiIsInR5cCI6IkpXVCJ9.eyJpc3MiOiJzdXBhYmFzZSIsInJlZiI6ImlqcGZjeWdwY3B2aWtncnRsa3dyIiwicm9sZSI6ImFub24iLCJpYXQiOjE3NTI2MzUwNjAsImV4cCI6MjA2ODIxMTA2MH0.8QnlCxewNzcEbj1ZiOmKLGAxbY6xOhna3ZWp1cS6q54"

supabase: Client = create_client(SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_KEY)

def import_without_problem_columns():
    print("Loading CSV...")
    df = pd.read_csv("DOB_Permit_Fixed (2).csv", dtype=str, low_memory=False)
    print(f"Loaded {len(df)} rows")
    
    # Drop the 3 problematic columns
    problem_cols = ["Owner's House City", "Owner's House State", "Owner's House Zip Code"]
    df_clean = df.drop(columns=problem_cols, errors='ignore')
    
    print(f"Dropped problem columns. Remaining: {len(df_clean.columns)} columns")
    
    # Fill NaN values
    df_clean = df_clean.fillna('')
    
    # Convert to records
    records = df_clean.to_dict('records')
    
    # Insert in chunks
    chunk_size = 1000
    for i in range(0, len(records), chunk_size):
        chunk = records[i:i+chunk_size]
        print(f"Inserting chunk {i//chunk_size + 1}")
        
        try:
            result = supabase.table("dob_permit_issuance").insert(chunk).execute()
            print(f"✅ Success")
            time.sleep(1)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"❌ Error: {e}")
            break
    
    print("Import complete!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    import_without_problem_columns()
```

Claude: ## Create Import Script for Full File:

import_full_file.py

```
import pandas as pd
from supabase import create_client, Client
import time

# Your Supabase credentials
SUPABASE_URL = "https://ijpfcygpcpvikgrtlkwr.supabase.co"
SUPABASE_KEY = "eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiIsInR5cCI6IkpXVCJ9.eyJpc3MiOiJzdXBhYmFzZSIsInJlZiI6ImlqcGZjeWdwY3B2aWtncnRsa3dyIiwicm9sZSI6ImFub24iLCJpYXQiOjE3NTI2MzUwNjAsImV4cCI6MjA2ODIxMTA2MH0.8QnlCxewNzcEbj1ZiOmKLGAxbY6xOhna3ZWp1cS6q54"

supabase: Client = create_client(SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_KEY)

def import_full_file():
    file_path = "/Users/levikatzman/Downloads/DOB_Permit_Issuance_20250716.csv"
    
    print("Reading CSV in chunks (this is a large file)...")
    
    chunk_size = 10000  # Process 10K rows at a time
    chunk_count = 0
    
    # Read CSV in chunks to handle the large file
    for chunk in pd.read_csv(file_path, chunksize=chunk_size, dtype=str, low_memory=False):
        chunk_count += 1
        print(f"\nProcessing chunk {chunk_count} ({len(chunk)} rows)")
        
        # Keep only latest permit per BIN (like we did in Colab)
        if chunk_count == 1:
            # Initialize with first chunk
            df_latest = chunk.copy()
        else:
            # Combine with existing data
            df_combined = pd.concat([df_latest, chunk], ignore_index=True)
            
            # Sort and keep latest per BIN
            if 'Issuance Date' in df_combined.columns:
                df_combined['Issuance Date'] = pd.to_datetime(df_combined['Issuance Date'], errors='coerce')
                df_sorted = df_combined.sort_values(['Bin #', 'Issuance Date'], ascending=[True, False])
                df_latest = df_sorted.drop_duplicates(subset=['Bin #'], keep='first')
            else:
                df_latest = df_combined.drop_duplicates(subset=['Bin #'], keep='first')
        
        print(f"Current unique properties: {len(df_latest)}")
        
        # Import every 50K processed records
        if chunk_count % 5 == 0:
            print("Importing batch to Supabase...")
            import_batch(df_latest.tail(1000))  # Import last 1000 records
    
    # Import final batch
    print(f"\nFinal deduplication: {len(df_latest)} unique properties")
    import_final_batch(df_latest)

def import_batch(df_batch):
    # Fill NaN values
    df_clean = df_batch.fillna('')
    
    # Convert to records
    records = df_clean.to_dict('records')
    
    # Insert in small chunks
    chunk_size = 500
    for i in range(0, len(records), chunk_size):
        chunk = records[i:i+chunk_size]
        try:
            result = supabase.table("dob_permit_issuance").insert(chunk).execute()
            print(f"✅ Inserted {len(chunk)} records")
            time.sleep(0.5)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"❌ Error: {e}")
            break

def import_final_batch(df_final):
    print("Importing final deduplicated dataset...")
    
    # Fill NaN values
    df_clean = df_final.fillna('')
    
    # Convert to records
    records = df_clean.to_dict('records')
    
    # Insert in chunks
    chunk_size = 1000
    for i in range(0, len(records), chunk_size):
        chunk = records[i:i+chunk_size]
        print(f"Importing chunk {i//chunk_size + 1}/{(len(records)//chunk_size) + 1}")
        
        try:
            result = supabase.table("dob_permit_issuance").upsert(chunk).execute()
            print(f"✅ Success")
            time.sleep(1)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"❌ Error: {e}")
            break
    
    print("Import complete!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    import_full_file()
```

import_full_file.py

```
python3 import_full_file.py
```

1. Process the full 323MB file in chunks
2. Deduplicate as it goes (keep latest permit per BIN)
3. Import to Supabase in manageable batches

Claude: ## Quick Fix:

import_full_file_fixed.py

```
import pandas as pd
from supabase import create_client, Client
import time

# Your Supabase credentials
SUPABASE_URL = "https://ijpfcygpcpvikgrtlkwr.supabase.co"
SUPABASE_KEY = "eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiIsInR5cCI6IkpXVCJ9.eyJpc3MiOiJzdXBhYmFzZSIsInJlZiI6ImlqcGZjeWdwY3B2aWtncnRsa3dyIiwicm9sZSI6ImFub24iLCJpYXQiOjE3NTI2MzUwNjAsImV4cCI6MjA2ODIxMTA2MH0.8QnlCxewNzcEbj1ZiOmKLGAxbY6xOhna3ZWp1cS6q54"

supabase: Client = create_client(SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_KEY)

def import_full_file():
    file_path = "/Users/levikatzman/Downloads/DOB_Permit_Issuance_20250716.csv"
    
    print("Reading CSV in chunks...")
    
    chunk_size = 10000
    chunk_count = 0
    df_latest = None
    
    for chunk in pd.read_csv(file_path, chunksize=chunk_size, dtype=str, low_memory=False):
        chunk_count += 1
        print(f"\nProcessing chunk {chunk_count} ({len(chunk)} rows)")
        
        # Keep only latest permit per BIN
        if df_latest is None:
            df_latest = chunk.copy()
        else:
            df_combined = pd.concat([df_latest, chunk], ignore_index=True)
            
            # Sort by BIN and date (keep as string, don't convert to datetime)
            if 'Issuance Date' in df_combined.columns:
                df_sorted = df_combined.sort_values(['Bin #', 'Issuance Date'], ascending=[True, False])
                df_latest = df_sorted.drop_duplicates(subset=['Bin #'], keep='first')
            else:
                df_latest = df_combined.drop_duplicates(subset=['Bin #'], keep='first')
        
        print(f"Current unique properties: {len(df_latest)}")
    
    # Import final deduplicated dataset
    print(f"\nFinal import: {len(df_latest)} unique properties")
    import_final_batch(df_latest)

def import_final_batch(df_final):
    print("Importing final deduplicated dataset...")
    
    # Convert all data to strings to avoid timestamp issues
    df_clean = df_final.astype(str).fillna('')
    
    # Convert to records
    records = df_clean.to_dict('records')
    
    # Insert in chunks
    chunk_size = 1000
    total_chunks = (len(records) + chunk_size - 1) // chunk_size
    
    for i in range(0, len(records), chunk_size):
        chunk = records[i:i+chunk_size]
        chunk_num = (i // chunk_size) + 1
        print(f"Importing chunk {chunk_num}/{total_chunks}")
        
        try:
            result = supabase.table("dob_permit_issuance").insert(chunk).execute()
            print(f"✅ Success - {len(chunk)} records")
            time.sleep(1)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"❌ Error: {e}")
            break
    
    print("Import complete!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    import_full_file()
```

Claude: `// Let's examine the data dictionary file
import * as XLSX from 'xlsx';

try {
    const dataDict = await window.fs.readFile('DD_DOB_Permit_Issuance_20190729 2.xlsx');
    const workbook = XLSX.read(dataDict);
    
    console.log("Worksheets in data dictionary:", workbook.SheetNames);
    
    // Get the Column Info sheet if it exists
    const columnInfoSheet = workbook.Sheets['Column Info'] || workbook.Sheets[workbook.SheetNames[0]];
    const columnData = XLSX.utils.sheet_to_json(columnInfoSheet, { header: 1 });
    
    console.log("\nFirst 20 rows of data dictionary:");
    columnData.slice(0, 20).forEach((row, i) => {
        if (row && row.length > 0) {
            console.log(`Row ${i}: ${row.join(' | ')}`);
        }
    });
    
    // Look for actual column definitions
    console.log("\nLooking for column definitions...");
    columnData.forEach((row, i) => {
        if (row && row[0] && typeof row[0] === 'string' && row[0].length > 2 && !row[0].includes('Column') && !row[0].includes('Field')) {
            console.log(`${i}: ${row[0]} | ${row[1] || ''}`);
        }
    });
    
} catch (error) {
    console.log("Error reading data dictionary:", error.message);
}`

Worksheets in data dictionary: ["Dataset Info","Column Info","Type Values","Dataset Revision History","Dropdown Options"]

First 20 rows of data dictionary:
Row 0: Data Dictionary - Column Information
Row 1: Column Name | Column Description | Additional Notes 
(where applicable, include the  range of possible values, units of measure, how to interpret null/zero values, whether there are specific relationships between columns, and information on column source) | Data Type (Plain Text, Number, Date) | Required
Row 2: BOROUGH | The name of the NYC borough where the proposed work will take place. | Expected values = Manhattan, Bronx, Brooklyn, Queens, Staten Island. | Text | Yes
Row 3: Bin # | Building Identification Number assigned by Department of City Planning. |  | Number | Yes
Row 4: House # | The house number for the building where the proposed work will take place. | In the address, "280 Broadway", 280 is the house number. This number may contain a dash (-), as in "8-15 27 Avenue". | Text
Row 5: Street Name | The street name for the building where the proposed work will take place. | In the address, "1050 Park Place", Park Place is the street name.  | Text | Yes
Row 6: Job # | The DOB Job Application Number assigned when the applicant begins the application. | This is the unique identifier for the application submitted to the Department. It may contain several work types, and more work types may be added as the application review and the work continues. It is a 9-digit number where the first digit indicates the borough where the building is located. 
• 1 = Manhattan
• 2 = Bronx
• 3 = Brooklyn
• 4 = Queens
• 5 = Staten Island | Number | Yes
Row 7: Job doc. # | A sequential number assigned to each of the documents that make up a job application. | Every job application should have a 01 document. Every additional document receives a number that increases by 1 (ex: 02, 03, 04) | Text | Yes
Row 8: Job Type | 2-digit code to indicate the overall job type for the application.

Note: You should also look at the Permit Type field to find out what this specific permit has been issued for. An NB job, for example, can have several different Work/Permit Types such as PL, SP, EQ, etc. And each Work/Permit Type will be issued a seperate permit. | Expected values are:
• A1 = Alteration Type I, A major alteration that will change the use, egress, or occupancy of the building.
• A2 = Alteration Type II, An application with multiple types of work that do not affect the use, egress, or occupancy of the building.
• A3 = Alteration Type III, One type of minor work that doesn't affect the use, egress, or occupancy of the building.
• NB = New Building, An application to build a new structure. “NB” cannot be selected if any existing building elements are to remain—for example a part of
an old foundation, a portion of a façade that will be incorporated into the construction, etc.
• DM = Demolition, An application to fully or partially demolish an existing building.
• SG = Sign, An application to install or remove an outdoor sign. | Text | Yes
Row 9: Self_Cert | Indicates whether or not the application was submitted as Professionally Certified. A Professional Engineer (PE) or Registered Architect (RA) can certify compliance with applicable laws and codes on applications filed by him/her as applicant. | Expected values are: 
• Y = Yes. 
• N = No.
• blank = No information for this record. | Text | No
Row 10: Block | Tax Block assigned to the location of the proposed work by the Department of Finance. |  | Text | Yes
Row 11: Lot | Tax Lot assigned to the location of the proposed work by the Department of Finance. | Note: NYC Tax Lots are 4 digits. This dataset reports them in 5 digits with a leading zero. If you are concatenating fields to create the BBL, you will need to adjust for this. | Text | Yes
Row 12: Community Board | Community Board Number for the building’s address. |  | Text | Yes
Row 13: Zip Code | ZIP Code for the building's address. |  | Text | No
Row 14: Bldg Type | Legal occupancy classification. | Expected values are:
• 1 = 1-2-3 Family
• 2 = Other

DOF classification has 36 types - this field just simplifies it for DOB purposes. Anything above 3 family we consider multiple dwelling and is regulated more heavily. Other agencies might have different cutoffs. | Text | No
Row 15: Residential |  | Expected values are: 
• Y = Yes. 
• blank = No information for this record. | Text | No
Row 16: Special District 1 |  |  | Text | No
Row 17: Special District 2 |  |  | Text | No
Row 18: Work Type | The specific type of work covered by the permit. | This is a two character code to indicate the type of work. Check the Type Values sheet in this document for expected values and definitions. If the Work Type is blank, check the Permit Type. | Text | No
Row 19: Permit Status | The current status of the permit application.  | Expected values are 
• IN PROCESS = The applicant submitted the PW-2 appliction for a permit and it is being processed
• ISSUED = The permit has been issued
• RE-ISSUED = The permit has been reissued
• REVOKED = The permit has been revoked | Text | Yes

This is copilot:
This is a fantastic summary—clear, detailed, and technically precise. You've framed the issue in a way that invites targeted solutions from other LLMs or developers familiar with Supabase and PostgREST quirks. To help you move forward, here are some additional suggestions and clarifications that might unlock a workaround or deeper fix:
🔧 Advanced Workarounds & Suggestions
1. Force Schema Reload via PostgREST
Even though pg_notify('pgrst', 'reload schema') is the documented method, it sometimes fails silently in managed environments like Supabase. Try:
* Restarting the Supabase project from the dashboard (this forces a full schema reload).
* Renaming the table temporarily, then renaming it back. This can trick PostgREST into refreshing its cache.
* Creating a dummy column, then dropping it—this may trigger a schema diff.
2. Avoid Apostrophes in Column Names (Temporarily)
PostgREST can be finicky with quoted identifiers containing apostrophes. Consider:
* Renaming problematic columns to temporary safe names (e.g., "Owner_House_City") during import.
* Use a view to map back to the original names for API compatibility:

pg_notify('pgrst', 'reload schema')

"Owner_House_City"

```
CREATE VIEW dob_permit_issuance_api AS
SELECT
  "Owner_House_City" AS "Owner's House City",
  ...
FROM dob_permit_issuance;
```

This preserves the original names for API consumers while avoiding schema cache issues.
3. Use psql COPY or pg_bulkload
Supabase allows SQL access via the SQL editor or external tools. You can:
* Use psql with the COPY command:

psql COPY

pg_bulkload

psql

COPY

```
psql -h your-host -U your-user -d your-db -c "\COPY dob_permit_issuance FROM 'yourfile.csv' WITH CSV HEADER"
```

- Or use pg_bulkload for faster ingestion if you have access to the underlying instance (less likely on Supabase).
- Use Supabase Storage + Server-Side Import Upload the CSV to Supabase Storage, then write a PostgreSQL function or Edge Function to read and insert the data. This avoids client-side schema cache issues.
- Check PostgREST Version Supabase uses PostgREST under the hood. Schema cache bugs have been fixed in newer versions. If you're on an older version, Supabase support might be able to upgrade it.

```
# Complete code to process data and create mapped CSV
from google.colab import files
import pandas as pd

# Upload your file
uploaded = files.upload()

## Alternative: Let's Go Back to Python

### Create a new file import_mapped.py:

import_mapped.py

```
import pandas as pd
from supabase import create_client, Client
import time

# Your Supabase credentials
SUPABASE_URL = "https://ijpfcygpcpvikgrtlkwr.supabase.co"
SUPABASE_KEY = "eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiIsInR5cCI6IkpXVCJ9.eyJpc3MiOiJzdXBhYmFzZSIsInJlZiI6ImlqcGZjeWdwY3B2aWtncnRsa3dyIiwicm9sZSI6ImFub24iLCJpYXQiOjE3NTI2MzUwNjAsImV4cCI6MjA2ODIxMTA2MH0.8QnlCxewNzcEbj1ZiOmKLGAxbY6xOhna3ZWp1cS6q54"

supabase: Client = create_client(SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_KEY)

def import_mapped_data():
    print("Loading mapped CSV...")
    df = pd.read_csv("DOB_Permit_Mapped.csv", dtype=str, low_memory=False)
    print(f"Loaded {len(df)} rows")
    
    # Fill NaN values
    df = df.fillna('')
    
    # Convert to records
    records = df.to_dict('records')
    
    # Import in chunks
    chunk_size = 1000
    total_chunks = (len(records) + chunk_size - 1) // chunk_size
    
    for i in range(0, len(records), chunk_size):
        chunk = records[i:i+chunk_size]
        chunk_num = (i // chunk_size) + 1
        print(f"Importing chunk {chunk_num}/{total_chunks}")
        
        try:
            result = supabase.table("dob_permit_issuance").insert(chunk).execute()
            print(f"✅ Success - {len(chunk)} records")
            time.sleep(1)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"❌ Error: {e}")
            break
    
    print("Import complete!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    import_mapped_data()
```

python3 import_mapped.py
```

## Create the Python File:

### Option 1: Use Terminal to create the file

```
cat > import_mapped.py << 'EOF'
import pandas as pd
from supabase import create_client, Client
import time

SUPABASE_URL = "https://ijpfcygpcpvikgrtlkwr.supabase.co"
SUPABASE_KEY = "eyJhbGciOiJIUzI1NiIsInR5cCI6IkpXVCJ9.eyJpc3MiOiJzdXBhYmFzZSIsInJlZiI6ImlqcGZjeWdwY3B2aWtncnRsa3dyIiwicm9sZSI6ImFub24iLCJpYXQiOjE3NTI2MzUwNjAsImV4cCI6MjA2ODIxMTA2MH0.8QnlCxewNzcEbj1ZiOmKLGAxbY6xOhna3ZWp1cS6q54"

supabase: Client = create_client(SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_KEY)

def import_mapped_data():
    print("Loading mapped CSV...")
    df = pd.read_csv("DOB_Permit_Mapped.csv", dtype=str, low_memory=False)
    print(f"Loaded {len(df)} rows")
    
    df = df.fillna('')
    records = df.to_dict('records')
    
    chunk_size = 1000
    total_chunks = (len(records) + chunk_size - 1) // chunk_size
    
    for i in range(0, len(records), chunk_size):
        chunk = records[i:i+chunk_size]
        chunk_num = (i // chunk_size) + 1
        print(f"Importing chunk {chunk_num}/{total_chunks}")
        
        try:
            result = supabase.table("dob_permit_issuance").insert(chunk).execute()
            print(f"✅ Success - {len(chunk)} records")
            time.sleep(1)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"❌ Error: {e}")
            break
    
    print("Import complete!")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    import_mapped_data()
EOF
```

```
python3 import_mapped.py
```

Claude: ## Step 19: Build Cross-Referencing Queries

### QUERY 24 (New Query) - Qualified Leads Query

```
-- Create a view that cross-references all data to find qualified leads
CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW qualified_leads AS
WITH recent_projects AS (
  -- Get recent DOB NOW applications for 10+ unit buildings
  SELECT DISTINCT
    app.bin,
    app.borough,
    app.block,
    app.lot,
    app.house_number,
    app.street_name,
    app.zip_code,
    app.job_number,
    app.job_description,
    app.work_type,
    app.applicant_business_name,
    app.applicant_first_name,
    app.applicant_last_name,
    app.applicant_license_number,
    app.filing_date,
    app.total_construction_floor_area,
    app.horizontal_enlargement,
    app.vertical_enlargement,
    app.estimated_job_costs
  FROM dob_now_applications app
  WHERE 
    app.filing_date >= CURRENT_DATE - INTERVAL '6 months'
    AND (
      app.job_description ILIKE '%unit%'
      OR app.job_description ILIKE '%apartment%'
      OR app.job_description ILIKE '%dwelling%'
      OR app.job_description ILIKE '%residential%'
      OR app.total_construction_floor_area::numeric > 10000
      OR app.estimated_job_costs::numeric > 500000
    )
),

building_info AS (
  -- Get building registration info
  SELECT 
    reg.bin,
    reg.house_number,
    reg.street_name,
    reg.borough,
    reg.zip_code,
    reg.block,
    reg.lot,
    reg.num_of_stories,
    reg.units_res AS residential_units,
    reg.year_built,
    reg.building_class,
    reg.owner_type,
    reg.owner_name,
    reg.owner_house_number,
    reg.owner_house_street,
    reg.owner_house_city,
    reg.owner_house_state,
    reg.owner_house_zip_code
  FROM multiple_dwelling_registrations reg
  WHERE reg.units_res::numeric >= 10
),

management_status AS (
  -- Check if building has management company
  SELECT 
    reg.bin,
    reg.registration_id,
    COUNT(cont.id) as management_contacts,
    MAX(cont.business_name) as current_management_company,
    MAX(cont.business_house_number || ' ' || cont.business_street_name) as management_address,
    MAX(cont.business_phone) as management_phone
  FROM multiple_dwelling_registrations reg
  LEFT JOIN registration_contacts cont ON reg.registration_id = cont.registration_id
  WHERE cont.contact_type = 'CorporateOwner' 
     OR cont.contact_type = 'Agent'
     OR cont.contact_type = 'Manager'
  GROUP BY reg.bin, reg.registration_id
),

owner_contact_info AS (
  -- Get owner contact info from approved permits
  SELECT DISTINCT
    approved.bin,
    approved.owner_business_name,
    approved.owner_first_name,
    approved.owner_last_name,
    approved.owner_phone,
    approved.issuance_date,
    ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY approved.bin ORDER BY approved.issuance_date DESC) as rn
  FROM dob_now_approved_permits approved
  WHERE approved.owner_business_name IS NOT NULL 
     OR (approved.owner_first_name IS NOT NULL AND approved.owner_last_name IS NOT NULL)
)

-- Final qualified leads output
SELECT 
  proj.bin,
  proj.borough,
  proj.house_number || ' ' || proj.street_name as address,
  proj.zip_code,
  proj.job_number,
  proj.job_description,
  proj.work_type,
  proj.filing_date,
  proj.estimated_job_costs,
  
  -- Building info
  bldg.residential_units,
  bldg.num_of_stories,
  bldg.year_built,
  bldg.building_class,
  bldg.owner_name as registered_owner,
  
  -- Owner contact (from permits)
  owner.owner_business_name as permit_owner_business,
  owner.owner_first_name as permit_owner_first,
  owner.owner_last_name as permit_owner_last,
  owner.owner_phone as permit_owner_phone,
  
  -- Management status
  COALESCE(mgmt.management_contacts, 0) as has_management,
  mgmt.current_management_company,
  mgmt.management_phone,
  
  -- Lead qualification score
  CASE 
    WHEN bldg.residential_units::numeric >= 50 THEN 5
    WHEN bldg.residential_units::numeric >= 25 THEN 4  
    WHEN bldg.residential_units::numeric >= 15 THEN 3
    WHEN bldg.residential_units::numeric >= 10 THEN 2
    ELSE 1
  END +
  CASE 
    WHEN proj.estimated_job_costs::numeric > 1000000 THEN 3
    WHEN proj.estimated_job_costs::numeric > 500000 THEN 2
    WHEN proj.estimated_job_costs::numeric > 100000 THEN 1
    ELSE 0
  END +
  CASE 
    WHEN COALESCE(mgmt.management_contacts, 0) = 0 THEN 3  -- No management = higher score
    WHEN COALESCE(mgmt.management_contacts, 0) = 1 THEN 1
    ELSE 0
  END as lead_score,
  
  -- Contact priority
  CASE
    WHEN COALESCE(mgmt.management_contacts, 0) = 0 AND bldg.residential_units::numeric >= 25 THEN 'HIGH'
    WHEN COALESCE(mgmt.management_contacts, 0) = 0 AND bldg.residential_units::numeric >= 15 THEN 'MEDIUM'
    WHEN COALESCE(mgmt.management_contacts, 0) <= 1 AND bldg.residential_units::numeric >= 10 THEN 'LOW'
    ELSE 'RESEARCH'
  END as contact_priority,
  
  CURRENT_TIMESTAMP as generated_at

FROM recent_projects proj
LEFT JOIN building_info bldg ON proj.bin = bldg.bin
LEFT JOIN management_status mgmt ON proj.bin = mgmt.bin  
LEFT JOIN owner_contact_info owner ON proj.bin = owner.bin AND owner.rn = 1

WHERE bldg.residential_units::numeric >= 10
ORDER BY lead_score DESC, bldg.residential_units::numeric DESC;
```

- ✅ Finds recent projects with 10+ units
- ✅ Cross-references building registrations
- ✅ Checks management status
- ✅ Gets owner contact info from permits
- ✅ Scores and prioritizes leads

```

### What We've Tried:

1. Supabase CSV import - Column name mismatch errors
2. Python bulk insert - Schema cache error persists
3. Recreated table multiple times with exact column names
4. Used pg_notify('pgrst', 'reload schema') - Cache won't refresh
5. Renamed columns to snake_case - Same error
6. Created new table with different name - Same error
7. Tried psql COPY command - Connection string issues with special characters in password

`pg_notify('pgrst', 'reload schema')`
